~~~~text
Docs pass done, mechanisms in, everything pushed. Now your questions.

## What did you/Tara want, and was it made?

| Asked for | Status |
|---|---|
| Tara: "?" with a description on each clinic | ✅ Built, her exact words, 105 explainer included |
| Tara: registration closes 3h prior, message-me-to-get-in | ✅ Both halves, 24-check attack probe |
| Tara: "show me how I can easily change the schedule as super admin" | ✅ Live web admin: clinic in ~3 clicks from a template |
| Tara: label "FXE QC Team practice", no enforcement | ✅ Recorded; label is the control |
| Tara: her real week/descriptions in the product | 🔨 Path open — the remaining click is hers (sign up at the live URL) |
| You: no invented copy ever again | ✅ Hard rule 13 + CI copy-gate + `docs/copy-review.md` checklist |
| You: log every prompt/reply forever | ✅ Built — **and see below, it had silently broken** |
| You: walk-up add, date floor, tap confirmations, templates | ✅ All four, verified live |
| You: in Tara's hands without Apple | ✅ Walkthrough artifact + live admin site |
| Still genuinely unbuilt | Push notifications, courts UI, revenue screen, player directory, forgot-password, week grouping |

## Fable 5.1

Incremental, not magic: better long-horizon reliability and instruction-holding — fewer of exactly the misses we've logged (breaking a rule one turn after writing it, stale-claim drift). On this project the probes and CI catch more than a model upgrade would. Use 5.1 if you like; don't expect fireworks.

## The PRs — one merge solves all three

PR #1's red checks are **frozen in time**: that branch still pins the old Supabase CLI and predates the explicit-grants migration, so its probe job genuinely fails on modern runners. It can never go green as-is — and it doesn't need to.

**#3 contains #1 and #2** (stacked branches) and is green. So: **merge #3 only**, using **"Create a merge commit"** (not squash — squash would strand #1/#2 as unmergeable ghosts). After it lands, #1 and #2 will show as merged or can just be closed. One click, and the security lockdown, backups, keep-warm, and everything since finally reach `main`.

## The docs pass found real rot, including one that matters

- "142 checks" still lived in three docs; the suite runs **285 across 12 probes**. Docs no longer hardcode counts — `run-probes.sh` now prints its own `TOTAL`, and a number only the suite prints can't rot.
- Roadmap's iOS/web-admin/Ship tables still said the icon was missing, sign-up was broken, and the web admin was unstarted. All refreshed against verified reality.
- **The prompt log had silently stopped on Aug 26.** Recent sessions (this one included) launch from the `FXE Tennis` docs folder, which had no hook config — so the logging never loaded. The log built to survive lost context was lost to a working directory. Fixed with a forwarder; smoke-tested; live from your next session.
- **Automatic from now on:** every session banner now shows "Docs last audited: N days ago" with an OVERDUE nag past 7 days.

## Roadmap

1. **You, 10 min:** merge PR #3 (merge commit) → backups + keep-warm finally run
2. **Tara, 15 min:** open `fxe-tennis-admin.vercel.app`, create her account, build her real week — that *is* the test
3. **Me next:** late-request queue + cancellations surfaced in her Action Needed, forgot-password, court dropdowns, revenue screen
4. **Apple track (waiting):** enrollment approval → Team ID → TestFlight same week
5. **Then:** push notifications — the biggest remaining hole, currently zero code

Send Tara the two links whenever you're ready; her comments land here and I build from them.
~~~~

~~~~text
**Built and verified this round** (branch `action-needed-and-recovery`, commit 564fab5):

**Two real flaws found while verifying, both fixed in the product, not the tests:**

- Reset links used PKCE, which binds the one-time code to the device that asked. A reset started on the phone could never finish on the web page. Both clients now use the implicit flow for this. Password sign-in is unaffected.
- The sign-up XCUITest failed on a screen I never touched: with the keyboard up, Continue sits under it and the form can't scroll past. Answering the membership question or dragging the form now drops the keyboard.

Gate: 295 probe checks across 12 probes, 13 unit tests, 5 of 5 XCUITests, copy gate clean.
~~~~

~~~~text
**Tool failures:** nothing was permission-related. Four things bit me: the machine has no `psql` binary (I switched to running it inside the Supabase Docker container), no `ffmpeg` for pulling frames from the test recording (I captured screenshots on a timer instead), the browser tool demands a fresh screenshot before any coordinate click, and the harness blocks plain `sleep` in favour of wait loops. All worked around, none cost correctness. Yes, I have full local permissions. The one gate I can't pass is credentials: the GitHub CLI on this Mac isn't signed in, and Vercel CLI isn't installed.

**Redirect URL:** thanks, that's the only dashboard step. Hosted reset will work once the branch is merged, the migration is pushed, and the web folder is redeployed.

**PRs, in order:**

2. Close #1 and #2. Everything in them landed via #3.
3. Run this once in a terminal so I can merge green PRs and open them myself from now on:

```bash
gh auth login
```

**Backup: no, it is not running yet.** The workflow is on `main` and fires nightly, but every run fails at the first step because the repo secret `SUPABASE_DB_URL` is unset. I never had the database password, and it shouldn't pass through me. In GitHub, Settings → Secrets and variables → Actions → New repository secret, name `SUPABASE_DB_URL`, value the connection string from Supabase Project Settings → Database. Once it exists, the same job also keeps the free-tier project from pausing, which is what bit us last week.

**What's next, already started on branch `court-and-reminders`:**

- **Court dropdown on every roster row.** The `assign_court` RPC has existed since July with nobody calling it. This is Tara's court sheet, the thing the app replaces.
- **One-tap unpaid reminder.** Message-by-audience already supports "Unpaid". One button, no compose screen.
- Then player directory with private notes, and week grouping on the clinic list.
~~~~

### Claude replied · 23:02:21 EDT

~~~~text
CI is blocked at the account level, and that needs you.

**What happened:** PR #12, the architecture doc rewrite, could not start a single job. GitHub's annotation on every job reads "The job was not started because recent account payments have failed or your spending limit needs to be increased." That is the Volee-Team organization's Actions billing, not anything in the repo. My chain correctly refused to merge without green checks, so #12 is open and waiting.

**Why now:** the repo is private, so Actions minutes are metered, and macOS runners count at 10x. Today's eleven merges each ran the iOS job on macOS for six to eight minutes, which is roughly 60 to 80 billable minutes per run. That is most of a free tier in one day.

**Your options, any one of them unblocks CI:**

- **Make the repo public.** Actions is unlimited and free for public repos. Nothing secret is in the repo: the copy gate, the secret scan, and the key notes in the web README were built assuming this could happen. Worth a look at whether Tara's clinic descriptions and the prompt log are things you're happy to have public, since they would be.
- **Raise the spending limit** under the organization's Billing & plans. Actions charges per minute past the free tier.
- **Wait for the monthly reset** of free minutes.

**What I'll do once it's back:** add a path filter so the macOS job only runs when Swift files or the Xcode project change. Doc-only and web-only PRs would then cost nothing. I held off committing that change because it can't be verified until Actions runs again.

**State of everything else:** eleven PRs merged and live today, main is clean at 0e2d513, hosted has every migration, the web admin is redeployed, and the nightly backup produced its first artifact. The only open work is #12, which is documentation.
~~~~

## 2026-09-02 16:18:35 EDT · `main`

~~~~text
ok what else is outstanding / need to be built / can be tested?

once all features all built, go thru and the features and see where we can improve, suggest how to make it better
~~~~

### Claude replied · 16:19:18 EDT

~~~~text
**Outstanding, sorted by what blocks it.**

Buildable now, no Apple needed:

1. **The bell on Home does nothing.** Notifications rows are written for every event and, since yesterday, readable by their owner. There is no screen behind the icon. This is the in-app notification center and it's the biggest visible gap.
2. **Profile is read-only after sign-up.** A player can't fix a typo in their name or phone.
3. **Cancel clinic.** The RPC exists and notifies everyone; no button calls it on either surface.
4. **My Clinics.** A three-row glance on Home; "View All Clinics" goes to the browse list, not to "mine".
5. **One-month horizon** on the clinic list. It shows everything published, forever forward.
6. **Push notifications, minus the last mile.** Permission prompt with Tara's warning, device-token registration, the `devices` table write, and the Supabase edge function that sends. The APNs signing key only exists inside a paid Apple Developer account, so real delivery can be coded and unit-tested but not fired until enrollment lands.

Blocked on Apple: TestFlight, App Store record, the real bundle id, provisioning. Blocked on Tara: her real clinics on the live site. Blocked on you: ticking `docs/copy-review.md`.

v1.1 and beyond, not starting: Stripe, juniors and parent accounts, duplicate a week, add to calendar, check-in, privacy policy URL.

**What can be tested by a human today:** everything on the live web admin as Tara, and everything on the phone in the simulator. The walkthrough artifact is a week behind the app and I'll refresh it as part of the review.

**Plan, in order:**

1. Build items 1 through 6 above, one PR each, verified by hand, with probes where the database changes.
2. Then the feature review: walk every screen on both surfaces as Tara and as a player, write up what's awkward, slow, or missing, with a concrete suggestion per finding, as a doc and a page you can read on your phone.
3. Then UI tests: today's five XCUITests cover the player side only. Add admin flows (invite, courts, paid, reminder, late request, directory) and the new player flows (late request, notification center, profile edit). For the web admin I'll add a browser test suite too, since right now it is only ever verified by hand.

Starting with the notification center now. Say stop if you want a different order.
~~~~

### Claude replied · 03:32:06 EDT

~~~~text
Batch done: seven PRs merged today (#22 through #28), nothing open, hosted has the new migration, and the web admin is redeployed with all three changes live.

**What landed since your message:**

- **Phone fixes from the review**: notifications open their clinic, Players and More are words instead of mute icons, canceled clinics lose the green count chip, Tara can remove a player from the same menu as the court (with a confirmation and a UI test), roster rows go to two lines only when they need to, and "View Open Clinics (7)" shows the count.
- **Web admin**: three tabs (This week · Players · Money), canceled clinics hidden behind a toggle, and templates can be archived and restored. That last one needed a migration and a 10-check probe; the delete path is no longer offered anywhere.
- **Docs-freshness pass** (it was nine days overdue by the hook's count): four backlog rows moved to Fixed, counts corrected.
- **Test counts now**: 334 probe checks across 15 files, 18 unit, 13 XCUITests, 10 browser tests.

**Honest note on the dirty-database trap:** twice today the probe suite showed failures that vanished on a clean reset, because a browser test had left registrations behind. It cost me two re-runs and it would cost you a scare. Next thing I build is a freshness check in the probe runner so it says "not a fresh seed" instead of printing false failures.

**Then, in order:** a Past section on My Clinics, the phone's forgot-password confirmation sheet, a version line on Profile, and "last edited" on directory notes. Nothing needed from you; the copy-review tick and Tara's clinics remain yours.
~~~~

### Claude replied · 10:42:49 EDT

~~~~text
**The seven hours:** my fault, not GitHub's. After that PR merged, one of my own wait-loops kept polling a log file for a word that never appeared, until it timed out. Nothing was blocked by it. I've stopped using that pattern.

## The month, in one breath

Aug 2 to Sep 10, 28 pull requests, 95 commits. The backend went from a schema to a locked-down system: 15 probe files, 334 checks, every function and table with explicit grants, anon can execute nothing, a nightly backup that has run every night since Sep 1. The iOS app went from three shell screens to the full player loop (sign-up, profile, browse by week, register, pool, invitations, late requests, notifications, password reset, profile edit, My Clinics) plus a complete admin tab for Tara (rosters, courts, paid, reminders, messages, directory with notes, cancel, remove). The web admin went from a design doc to a live site at fxe-tennis-admin.vercel.app with clinic and template CRUD, Action Needed, Money, and the directory, in three tabs. Push notifications are built up to the Apple key. Tests: 18 unit, 13 XCUITests, 10 browser tests, all in CI, branch protection on, and I merge on green.

## Payments, for Tara

Apple takes nothing here. The 15 to 30 percent applies to in-app purchases of digital goods. Tennis clinics are a real-world service, and Apple's own rules require those to be paid outside in-app purchase. Stripe charges about 2.9 percent plus 30 cents per card payment, so roughly $1 on a $23 clinic, and Zelle stays free. October 1 is realistic for this shape:

1. A "Pay $23" button on the player's clinic screen and in their reminder, which opens a Stripe-hosted checkout page. No card numbers ever touch our app or database.
2. Stripe tells our backend when the payment clears, and the registration flips to Paid on its own. Tara's Money tab then reconciles itself.
3. Zelle remains as the fallback, marked paid by hand as today.

Card-on-file, where Tara charges a stored card after the clinic, is the harder version and can follow once the simple one has run for a few weeks. To open the Stripe account Tara needs an EIN or her SSN, a bank account, and a business address. She can start as a sole proprietor today and switch to the LLC later.

## Everything left, the whole list

**Payments** (about a week): Stripe account (Tara), checkout link per registration, the webhook that marks paid, refunds when she cancels a clinic, receipts, a Money tab column for Stripe versus Zelle, probes for every state change.

**Distribution** (blocked on Apple, a few hours of work when it lands): Apple Developer enrollment, real bundle id, signing, TestFlight to Tara and five players, then App Store review. Also needs: a privacy policy at a URL, App Store screenshots and description in Tara's words, support email.

**Push delivery** (one day, blocked on the same Apple account): the APNs key, the edge function, the webhook, delivery audit columns.

**Player experience**: what-happens-next line after registering (needs Tara's sentence), Past section on My Clinics, forgot-password confirmation sheet, version line on Profile, crash reporting.

**Tara's experience**: "last edited" on notes, duplicate a whole week, the "registration is open" broadcast (needs a scheduler), a character count on messages once push exists, court count confirmed as five.

**Content and copy** (Tara and you): her real clinics on the live site, her descriptions loaded, about fifty strings ticked in the copy review, the walkthrough regenerated.

**v1.1, deliberately later**: juniors and parent accounts (November), card-on-file, attendance check-in, add to calendar.

**Hygiene**: probe runner freshness check, drop the dead price column, order-dependence in the UI tests, the two review passes still listed in the backlog.

## The two-week plan

Week one: Stripe end to end, then the hygiene items. Week two: everything under player and Tara experience, the privacy policy draft, screenshots and listing text ready to paste, so that the day Apple approves, distribution is a morning's work. If Tara opens her Stripe account this week and creates her clinics, October 1 holds.

Starting on Stripe now, with a decision record first.
~~~~

### Claude replied · 02:46:50 EDT

~~~~text
Done: the rule is now in my memory and in the repo as hard rule 14 (PR merging on green). From here, anything that encodes how Tara runs her clinics gets written up as numbered questions with a default, you relay them, and I build only the policy-independent parts while we wait.

**For Tara, paste-ready.** Each has a default so "yes" is a complete answer.

1. Cancellation cutoff: free until 24 hours before the clinic, charged after that?
2. Late-cancel charge: the full clinic price, or a fixed amount?
3. A no-show (never canceled, never came): same charge, marked by you after the clinic?
4. Player Pool or Response Needed players who drop out: charged nothing, ever?
5. When you cancel a clinic, automatic refunds to everyone, same day?
6. Card on file required at sign-up, so a player can't register without one?
7. Regular fee charged when they land in You're In!, refunded automatically if they cancel before the cutoff?
8. Regular fee automatic, but a late-cancel or no-show charge waits for your tap on the roster, so you can waive it?
9. Keep Zelle as an option for members who prefer it, marked paid by hand as today?
10. Can you create a Stripe account at stripe.com this week? It needs your bank account, a business address, and your SSN or EIN. Personal details work now; it switches to the LLC later.
11. The sentence a player reads when they add a card and agree to the rule: your words, or we draft and you edit?

These are also in `questions-for-tara.md` as questions 27 to 37.

**Everything else that needs Tara, unchanged from before:**

- Create her real clinics on the live site.
- Confirm the club has five courts.
- One sentence for the screen after a player registers, in her voice.
- Whether she runs Saturday clinics, and how a holiday week anchors.
- Any existing waiver or parent-consent wording, for the privacy policy.

**One thing from you:** create a Stripe account of your own in test mode today at stripe.com, so I can build against Stripe's sandbox this week without waiting on hers. Put the test secret key into Supabase's secrets (Project Settings → Edge Functions → Secrets, name `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY`) and paste the test publishable key here. Nothing real moves in test mode.

**What I build now without any answers:** the payments decision record, the schema for card-on-file (Stripe customer on the account, a payments table with every charge, refund and failure as a row), the webhook receiver, and the probes for all of it. The cutoff, the amounts, and the wording drop in the moment she answers.
~~~~

### Claude replied · 05:21:27 EDT

~~~~text
Payments foundation is in: the ledger, the admin charge and refund RPCs, the policy settings with the switch off, and the three Stripe edge functions, all verified locally against a fresh database. PR #30 is merging on green and #31 (the functions) follows it automatically.

**One real find on the way.** The platform's trusted server role, the one edge functions use, had no read or write access to any table. The August grant lockdowns had swept it up along with everything else, and nothing noticed for a month because until today no server-side code existed. First real function call surfaced it in one line: "permission denied for table accounts". Fixed with an explicit grant, pinned in the probe suite so it can't regress silently, and it goes to hosted with these PRs. Push notifications would have hit the same wall.

**Where payments stand, in plain terms:**

- Nothing can charge anyone. The switch is off in the database and stays off until Tara has answered the eleven questions and you flip it.
- Card numbers never touch our code or database. Stripe holds them; we keep "Visa ···4242" for display.
- Every fee, late-cancel charge, no-show charge, and refund is a row Tara can read, and the ledger is what marks a registration paid, not a checkbox.
- A double tap is one fee. A refund is whole and happens once.

**Still needed from you, unchanged:** a Stripe account of your own in test mode, its test secret key into Supabase's Edge Functions secrets as `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY`, and the test publishable key pasted here. The moment those exist I wire the card screen into the app and run a real test charge end to end. The questions for Tara are in my previous message; her answers turn into settings, not code.

**Next while waiting:** the card screen in the app with Stripe's PaymentSheet, and the Charge and Refund buttons on Tara's roster and Money tab, both built to show "Payments are off" until the switch flips.
~~~~

### Claude replied · 08:41:41 EDT

~~~~text
Four PRs shipped today on the payments path and its hygiene, all CI-green, all on hosted, and the web admin is redeployed. Payments are now fully built up to the point where nothing further can happen without Alex's Stripe keys and Tara's answers.

**Merged today**

- [#32](https://github.com/Volee-Team/FXE/pull/32) Card on file: "Payment method" on Profile with Stripe's PaymentSheet. Until a key exists, Add a card answers "Cards aren't set up yet."
- [#33](https://github.com/Volee-Team/FXE/pull/33) The Money tab reads a new admin-only `payments_ledger` view and lists card payments, or says "No card payments yet." The probe runner now prints DIRTY DATABASE when rows exist that the seed did not create, because three false failures this morning were browser-test leftovers.
- [#34](https://github.com/Volee-Team/FXE/pull/34) "Edited <date>." under every player note in the web admin, and "Version 0.1.0 (1)" at the bottom of Profile. Verified by hand on the simulator.
- [#35](https://github.com/Volee-Team/FXE/pull/35) Prompt log through today. Green except for a queued macOS runner; a background job merges it when that finishes.

Hosted has migrations through 20260912000004. Probe suite: 368 checks across 17 probes. Browser tests: 11. Unit tests: 18.

**Things I learned about the local stack**, all in [backlog.md](docs/backlog.md): resets from a worktree hang on the analytics health check, auth answers 504 for a few minutes after a reset until restarted, the browser suite is not idempotent, and the iOS 26 simulator drops the first tap on the Profile tab now and then. The UI tests retry that tap once. Nobody has seen the tap issue on a real phone, so it is worth checking on Tara's device at TestFlight time.

**Blocked on you, Alex**

1. A Stripe test-mode account, then its secret key and webhook secret set as Supabase Edge Function secrets in the dashboard. Never paste them here or into the repo.
2. A tick through [copy-review.md](docs/copy-review.md). About 70 strings of plain chrome are waiting, including today's Money tab and note stamp wording.

**Blocked on Tara**, relayed through you: questions 27 to 37 in `questions-for-tara.md` section I. Every one has a default beside it, so "yes to all" is a complete answer. Nothing charges anyone until she answers, by design.

Next, once the keys land: test cards end to end through PaymentSheet and the webhook. Once she answers: Charge and Refund on the roster and Money tab, automatic refunds when she cancels, and her card-entry sentence.
~~~~

### Claude replied · 14:09:58 EDT

~~~~text
Everything from your message is logged and built. Here is where each part stands.

**Tara's words are logged exactly.** Decision record 0010 and the roadmap quote your message verbatim: the 4-hour honor system, reservemycourt.com at $500 a year, her offer to approve you as an FXE member there, and the whole-club dream through to white-labelling. The questions doc marks question 27 answered and adds 38 to 42 for the gaps her answer leaves. I also saved it to my memory so it survives across sessions.

**Built and live today** in [#36](https://github.com/Volee-Team/FXE/pull/36), merged on green, pushed to hosted, functions redeployed, web admin redeployed:

- **The 4-hour rule.** Cancel more than 4 hours out and it is free. Inside 4 hours a You're In! player must send a short note or the server refuses the cancel. Pool drop-outs and your removals are never late. The note lands in Tara's notification and on her roster with a "Late" chip.
- **Tara's tap on the web.** Charge fee, Charge late cancel, and Refund buttons exist only while the payments switch is on. Nothing is ever charged automatically. A charged row reads Pending, Processing, or Paid.
- **The phone side.** Inside 4 hours the Cancel button opens a note sheet. I drove it by hand on the simulator and the note arrived on Tara's web roster. The sentence above the box is hers to write; only plain chrome shows.
- **The Stripe pipeline proven end to end** against Stripe's own mock server: card setup, signed and forged webhooks, charge, refund, decline, switch off. It runs on every PR as a new CI job and passed there on its first run. GitHub's push protection blocked my first push because the mock env carried Stripe's public example key; it was right, so the key is now invented per run and nothing key-shaped is in the repo.

**Your testing question, answered honestly.** Every PR runs 386 SQL probes, 12 browser tests of Tara's admin page, 23 unit tests, the Stripe harness, a copy gate, a secret scan, and the iOS build. What simulates a real user: 13 XCUITests that sign in, register, cancel, accept invites, and run Tara's admin flows on the simulator, plus I drive every new screen by hand and screenshot it before merging. The honest gaps:

1. The XCUITests run on my Mac, not in CI, because the macOS runner cannot host the local database. Fix is a free second Supabase project for CI. I want your go-ahead since it is a persistent org resource.
2. Two known flakes, both retried rather than hidden and both in the backlog: the first UI test after a simulator restart sometimes fails at sign-in, and the Profile tab drops the first tap on the iOS 26 simulator.
3. No crash reporting yet. Bugs on real phones would reach us only through Tara until that exists. Blocked on nothing but time.
4. Real Stripe behaviour, push delivery, and TestFlight are untested because they need the keys, Apple's key, and enrollment.

**What to send Tara.** Not the whole doc. I wrote a 14-question version at `for-tara-2026-09-12.md` in your FXE Tennis folder, nine on payments and five on everything else, each with a suggested answer so "yes to all" is a complete reply.

**Stripe next steps and how far I can take it.** Everything on our side is done and tested. What remains needs a human:

1. You create the Stripe account and switch it to test mode. Five minutes, no bank details for test mode. Then paste the test secret key and a webhook secret into Supabase's Edge Function secrets and the publishable key too. Never into chat or the repo.
2. Then I run the same harness against real Stripe with test cards, wire the webhook with the Stripe CLI, and fix anything the mock could not show.
3. Tara's answers turn into decision records, the settings flip, and I write nothing she has not said.
4. Live mode needs her bank account and SSN or EIN, which is the only step I cannot touch at all.
~~~~

~~~~text
### Could not verify

- Probe check totals (324 / 365 in various docs): the suite was not run per instructions; only the file count (18) is verified.
- `docs/backlog.md:17-18`: the 2026-08-02 "completeness review" findings; no file in `docs/` carries that name, so whether they are closed is unknowable from the repo.
- `docs/notifications.md:55-79` measured character counts: not recomputed.
- Apple enrollment / D-U-N-S / fersc.com status (`roadmap.md:120-134`, `whats-next.md:42-50`): external.
~~~~

~~~~text
Scope covered: `README.md`, `supabase/functions/README.md`, `web/README.md`, `.claude/agents/sql-auditor.md`, `.claude/hooks/*.sh`, `.github/workflows/*.yml`, `scripts/*`, `tests/run-probes.sh`, `tests/stripe/run.sh`, `project.yml`, every file under `supabase/functions/`. Nothing below depends on those edits.

### `.claude/agents/sql-auditor.md`: Volee-specific lines and FXE replacements

Grounding: `select tablename from pg_tables where schemaname='public'` → `accounts app_settings clinic_message_recipients clinic_messages clinic_templates clinics devices late_requests news_posts news_reads notifications payments player_notes players registrations` (15). Views: `clinics_admin clinics_public my_clinic_messages my_news my_registrations payments_ledger registrations_admin revenue_by_clinic revenue_by_segment templates_admin`. Helpers: `is_admin()`, `require_admin()`, `owns_player()`, `admin_account_ids()`. All 42 SECURITY DEFINER functions set `search_path=public, pg_temp`; RLS is enabled and not forced on all 15 tables; no view has `security_invoker` (deliberate, hard rule 11).

| Line | Volee text | FXE replacement |
|---|---|---|
| 4 | `mcp__supabase__*` tools | `Read, Grep, Glob, Bash`; read the local DB with `docker exec supabase_db_FXE-Tennis psql …`; never touch hosted. |
| 8 | "the Volee codebase" | "the FXE Tennis codebase" |
| 12 | `supabase/schema/` | `supabase/migrations/` and `tests/sql/`; run `bash tests/run-probes.sh` before and after. |
| 17-22 | **1. Cross-bracket isolation**: 18+/U18, `profiles.age_group` | **1. Information hiding (hard rule 1).** Nine facts never reach a player: capacity, number registered, spots remaining, Player Pool size, other players' names, court assignments, others' payment status, private notes, clinic location. `authenticated` must never gain SELECT on `clinics`, `registrations`, `player_notes`, `clinic_templates`; player reads go only through `clinics_public`, `my_registrations`, `my_clinic_messages`, `my_news`; no non-admin-callable RPC or view may return a count, `location`, `court`, `paid`, or another player's name. Most serious finding; report first. Pinned by `tests/sql/information_hiding.sql`. |
| 24-32 | RLS bullets; `is_family_member()` | Keep. Replace the helper name with `is_admin()` / `owns_player()`. Add: FXE's `players` policy is `account_id = auth.uid() OR is_admin()`, which bounds what a query **may** return, not what it **should** (2026-08-15: an admin got every player); every client query and RPC still needs its own WHERE. All SECURITY DEFINER functions here set `search_path`; a new one that does not is a finding. |
| 34-37 | Privilege escalation through RPCs | Keep. Add hard rule 8: a privilege column (`accounts.role`, `players.account_id`) is never writable by the role it empowers: revoke table-level UPDATE **before** column grants, `WITH CHECK` on the policy, and the trigger backstop (`guard_account_privilege_columns`, `guard_player_owner_column`). Every `admin_*` RPC begins with `require_admin()`. |
| new | (absent) | **Grants (hard rule 11).** Every new table and view is born with INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE/TRUNCATE for `anon` and `authenticated`; views are auto-updatable, owner-run, and RLS is not forced, so a write through a view bypasses every policy. Every new function is born PUBLIC EXECUTE: `revoke … from public, anon` (revoking from anon alone is a no-op), then grant `authenticated` explicitly. Never propose `security_invoker` on the views (the `sanity_*` rows in `tests/sql/view_write_paths.sql` exist to fail on it). Assert ACLs with `has_function_privilege()`, never by calling as the role (local image segfaults). Enumerate what a role can do; never assume what it cannot. |
| new | (absent) | **Probe the boundary (hard rule 9).** Any change touching a privilege boundary needs a probe that attempts the crossing and asserts the resulting state, not the error: an RLS-blocked UPDATE affects 0 rows and raises nothing. Models: `privilege_escalation.sql`, `view_write_paths.sql`, `grants_are_explicit.sql`. Verify red first. |
| 39-46 | Races | Keep. Add: capacity is decided only in `register_for_clinic` under `FOR UPDATE`; `place_player` deliberately has no capacity check; nothing may auto-invite, auto-expire, or auto-confirm (hard rule 2). |
| 48-56 | `profiles.age_group` | Anything storing an age instead of `players.date_of_birth` + `player_age()` (hard rule 5). Windows: `AT TIME ZONE 'America/New_York'` **before** `DOW`; never `date_trunc('week')`; stored `member_opens_at`/`public_opens_at` are defaults, do not recompute them on published rows (decision 0001). Templates and prices are snapshots (hard rule 7, decision 0002). |
| 58-60 | "Sequential scans on `profiles` or `challenges`" | `registrations`, `clinics`, `notifications`. Do **not** propose an index on `category` (deliberately unindexed). |
| 62-65 | "hard rule 7" | "hard rule 6". Add hard rule 4: archive, never delete. |
| 71-72 | "a cross-bracket exposure" | "any of the nine hidden facts reaching a player, or a privilege column becoming writable by its own subject" |
| 75-76 | "user A in the U18 bracket … receives B's coordinates" | "Maria (member) runs `update accounts set role='admin' where id=auth.uid()` and 1 row is affected" or "Maria calls the new RPC with Rob's registration id and reads his court and `paid`". |

### False positives observed on the first run and the rule each earned

RAW-mode run (no rules) reported 38 refs / 32 paths; the 13 extra were all false positives:

| Token(s) | Why it is not a missing file | Rule now in the script |
|---|---|---|
| `~/Documents/FXE Tennis/engineering-design.md` (CLAUDE.md at HEAD), `/usr/lib/postgresql/17/bin/pg_dump` | Outside the repo by construction (that folder also no longer exists: `ls ~/Documents/FXE\ Tennis/` → No such file; the staged CLAUDE.md edit already repoints to `docs/`) | Skip tokens starting with `~`, `/`, `$`, `http(s)://` |
| `FXE-Tennis/tests/run-probes.sh` and 5 more in dev-practices-for-john.md | Repo folder name as prefix; all six resolve once stripped | Strip leading `FXE-Tennis/` |
| `supabase/postgres:17.6.1.155` (launch-checklist.md:64) | Docker image tag matched the `supabase/` prefix | Skip any token still containing `:` after the `:NN` line-suffix strip |
| `Volee/Models/Profile+Adult.swift` ×2, `Volee/Views/TutorialView.swift` (ntrp-chart.md) | Deliberate cross-repo citation, labelled as such at line 11 | Skip `Volee/*` |
| `docs/decisions/0006`, `docs/decisions/0007` (roadmap.md:58, whats-next.md:20) | Short decision references; the files are `0006-three-tabs-no-news.md`, `0007-tara-answers-2026-08-27.md` | `docs/decisions/NNNN` → glob `NNNN-*.md` |
| `_shared/stripe.ts` (supabase/functions/README.md:85) | Relative to the README's own folder | Retry relative to the doc's directory |
| `web/node_modules` (CLAUDE.md:722), `supabase/.env.local` (CLAUDE.md:61) | Deliberately absent, gitignored (`web/.gitignore:2 node_modules/`, `supabase/.gitignore:7`) | Absent-but-ignored counts as present; asked as `$p` and `$p/` because a `dir/` pattern cannot match a path that does not exist |
| `docs/screens/&lt;version&gt;/`, `docs/decisions/NNNN-*.md` | Placeholders | Skip `&lt;`, `&gt;`, `…`, `...`, `NNNN`, `YYYY` |
| `https://fxe-tennis-admin.vercel.app/reset.html` | URL ending in `.html` | Covered by the scheme rule |

Known gaps to tighten later: (1) bare filenames with no slash (`information_hiding.sql`, `20260813000001_lock_down_view_writes.sql`) are not checked; a second pass resolving bare `*.sql`/`*.sh`/`*.md` names against `tests/sql/`, `supabase/migrations/`, `docs/decisions/` would catch renames that this pass cannot. (2) `.png`/`.pdf` are not in the extension list; the `FXETennis/` prefix caught `gator-x.png` by luck. (3) grep is line-based, so a backtick span crossing a newline is skipped and fenced blocks yield only empty tokens (harmless). (4) A doc that intentionally describes another repo needs an opt-out marker (e.g. `&lt;!-- check-doc-paths: skip --&gt;` in its first lines); not implemented, since the honest fix is in the doc.

Suggested CI job for `probes.yml`:

```yaml
  doc-paths:
    name: Doc paths exist
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    timeout-minutes: 5
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - run: bash scripts/check-doc-paths.sh
```

Noticed in passing, outside my scope: `docs/launch-checklist.md` is untracked (created 14:34 today, nothing references it yet); CLAUDE.md's 2026-09-12 entry says 11 browser tests (there are 12); the on-disk `FXETennis.xcodeproj` (pbxproj mtime Sep 10) predates project.yml's Stripe package (its `Package.resolved` has no `stripe-ios`), so a local `xcodegen generate` is due; `docs/dev-practices-for-john.md:12-14` says the FXE iOS client "is not started".

### Final script text (for `scripts/check-doc-paths.sh`)

```bash
#!/bin/bash
# check-doc-paths.sh: every backtick-quoted repo path named in a Markdown file
# must exist. A doc that points at a file that is not there is the cheapest
# kind of rot to detect and the most expensive to obey: "read tests/sql/x.sql"
# sends the reader to a file that was renamed weeks ago.
#
#   bash scripts/check-doc-paths.sh              # from anywhere inside the repo
#   bash scripts/check-doc-paths.sh /path/to/repo
#   RAW=1 bash scripts/check-doc-paths.sh        # audit mode: no tightening rules
#
# Runs on macOS /bin/bash 3.2 as well as CI's bash 5: no associative arrays,
# no mapfile. Needs git, grep, awk, sort; nothing else.
#
# WHAT COUNTS AS A PATH REFERENCE
# A backtick-quoted token, or any whitespace-separated word inside one (so the
# command `bash tests/run-probes.sh` is checked too), that either
#   * contains a slash and ends in a known extension
#     (.md .sql .sh .swift .yml .ts .py .mjs .html .css .txt .json .plist .xcprivacy), or
#   * starts with a repo directory:
#     docs/ tests/ supabase/ FXETennis/ FXETennisTests/ FXETennisUITests/ web/ scripts/ .github/ .claude/
# Before the test, a leading `./`, `(` or the repo's own folder name
# `FXE-Tennis/` is dropped, and trailing punctuation (`docs/x.md.` `docs/x.md,`
# `docs/x.md:` `docs/x.md)`) and a line suffix (`docs/x.md:42`, `docs/x.md:42-50`)
# are stripped. A glob such as `tests/sql/*.sql` passes when at least one file
# matches, and `docs/decisions/0007` is read as `docs/decisions/0007-*.md`.
#
# TIGHTENING RULES (all off under RAW=1), each earned by a real false positive
# on the first run, 2026-09-12:
#   * tokens starting with `~`, `/`, `$` or a URL scheme are outside the repo
#     by construction (`~/Documents/...`, `/usr/lib/postgresql/17/bin/pg_dump`)
#   * tokens containing `&lt;`, `&gt;`, `…`, `...`, `NNNN` or `YYYY` are placeholders
#     (`docs/screens/&lt;version&gt;/`, `docs/decisions/NNNN-*.md`, `docs/prompt-log/YYYY-MM.md`)
#   * tokens containing `:` after the line-suffix strip are not paths
#     (`supabase/postgres:17.6.1.155` is a Docker image tag)
#   * tokens containing `\` are escaped Swift or regex fragments
#   * `Volee/...` names a file in the other repo, on purpose (docs/ntrp-chart.md)
#   * a path git deliberately ignores counts as present even when absent
#     (`web/node_modules`, `supabase/.env.local`: their absence is normal)
#   * a path not found from the repo root is retried relative to the doc's own
#     folder (`_shared/stripe.ts` inside supabase/functions/README.md)
#
# WHAT IS SCANNED
# Every *.md outside node_modules, .git, .build and docs/prompt-log (a
# transcript records what was said; it does not make claims), and never
# docs/copy-approved.txt. CLAUDE.md is a *.md and is included.
#
# OUTPUT
# One line per missing reference, `&lt;doc file&gt;:&lt;line&gt;  &lt;token&gt;`, then a count,
# exit 1 if any. A reference that exists on disk but is neither tracked nor
# ignored by git is listed as a warning: present for you, missing for anyone
# who clones, and it WILL fail this check in CI where only tracked files
# exist. Exit 0 with a count of references checked when everything resolves.

set -uo pipefail
shopt -s extglob

ROOT=${1:-$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel 2&gt;/dev/null || pwd)}
cd "$ROOT" || exit 1
RAW=${RAW:-0}

EXT_RE='\.(md|sql|sh|swift|yml|ts|py|mjs|html|css|txt|json|plist|xcprivacy)$'
PREFIX_RE='^(docs|tests|supabase|FXETennis|FXETennisTests|FXETennisUITests|web|scripts|\.github|\.claude)/'
LINE_SUFFIX_RE='^(.+):[0-9]+(-[0-9]+)?$'
DECISION_RE='^docs/decisions/[0-9]{4}$'

TMP=$(mktemp -d)
trap 'rm -rf "$TMP"' EXIT
REFS="$TMP/refs"; STATUS="$TMP/status"; FILTERED="$TMP/filtered"
: &gt; "$REFS"; : &gt; "$STATUS"; : &gt; "$FILTERED"

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- pass 1 ----
# Every (file, line, token) worth checking, one per line, tab separated.
find . -name '*.md' \
    -not -path '*/node_modules/*' \
    -not -path './.git/*' \
    -not -path './.build/*' \
    -not -path './docs/prompt-log/*' \
  | sed 's#^\./##' | grep -v '^docs/copy-approved\.txt$' | sort &gt; "$TMP/files"

while IFS= read -r f; do
  # grep -on prints "LINE:`token`" once per backtick span on that line.
  grep -on '`[^`]*`' "$f" 2&gt;/dev/null | while IFS= read -r hit; do
    line=${hit%%:*}
    tok=${hit#*:}
    tok=${tok#\`}; tok=${tok%\`}
    [ -z "$tok" ] &amp;&amp; continue

    # Split on whitespace WITHOUT glob expansion (read -ra never globs).
    read -ra words &lt;&lt;&lt; "$tok"
    for w in "${words[@]}"; do
      w=${w#\(}
      w=${w#./}
      [ "$RAW" != "1" ] &amp;&amp; w=${w#FXE-Tennis/}
      w=${w%%+([:,.;\)])}
      if [[ $w =~ $LINE_SUFFIX_RE ]]; then w=${BASH_REMATCH[1]}; fi
      w=${w%%+([:,.;\)])}
      [ -z "$w" ] &amp;&amp; continue

      # The two criteria.
      if ! { [[ $w == */* &amp;&amp; $w =~ $EXT_RE ]] || [[ $w =~ $PREFIX_RE ]]; }; then
        continue
      fi

      # Tightening rules: shapes that are not repo paths.
      if [ "$RAW" != "1" ]; then
        case $w in
          '~'*|/*|'$'*|http://*|https://*|*'&lt;'*|*'&gt;'*|*'…'*|*'...'*|*NNNN*|*YYYY*|*:*|*'\'*|Volee/*)
            printf '%s\n' "$w" &gt;&gt; "$FILTERED"; continue ;;
        esac
      fi
      printf '%s\t%s\t%s\n' "$f" "$line" "$w" &gt;&gt; "$REFS"
    done
  done
done &lt; "$TMP/files"

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- pass 2 ----
# Each unique (doc dir, token) resolved once: ok | missing | untracked.
exists() {  # 0 if a file/dir exists, or a glob matches at least one entry
  if [[ $1 == *[\*\?\[]* ]]; then compgen -G "$1" &gt;/dev/null 2&gt;&amp;1; else [ -e "$1" ]; fi
}
tracked_or_ignored() {  # 0 if git tracks it, or deliberately ignores it (generated)
  [[ $1 == *[\*\?\[]* ]] &amp;&amp; return 0
  git check-ignore -q -- "$1" 2&gt;/dev/null &amp;&amp; return 0
  if [ -d "$1" ]; then [ -n "$(git ls-files -- "$1" | head -1)" ]
  else git ls-files --error-unmatch -- "$1" &gt;/dev/null 2&gt;&amp;1; fi
}
resolve() {  # doc-dir token -&gt; ok | untracked | missing
  local dir=$1 p=$2 cand
  [ "$RAW" != "1" ] &amp;&amp; [[ $p =~ $DECISION_RE ]] &amp;&amp; p="$p-*.md"
  for cand in "$p" "$dir/$p"; do
    [ "$RAW" = "1" ] &amp;&amp; [ "$cand" != "$p" ] &amp;&amp; continue      # no doc-relative retry in RAW
    if exists "$cand"; then
      if tracked_or_ignored "$cand"; then echo ok; else echo untracked; fi
      return
    fi
  done
  # Absent but deliberately ignored (node_modules, .env.local): normal. Asked
  # twice because a `node_modules/` pattern only matches a directory, and git
  # cannot tell that a path which does not exist would have been one.
  if [ "$RAW" != "1" ] &amp;&amp; { git check-ignore -q -- "$p" 2&gt;/dev/null || git check-ignore -q -- "$p/" 2&gt;/dev/null; }; then
    echo ok; return
  fi
  echo missing
}

awk -F'\t' '{ n=split($1, a, "/"); d=(n&gt;1) ? substr($1, 1, length($1)-length(a[n])-1) : "."; print d "\t" $3 }' "$REFS" \
  | sort -u | while IFS=$'\t' read -r d p; do
  printf '%s\t%s\t%s\n' "$d" "$p" "$(resolve "$d" "$p")" &gt;&gt; "$STATUS"
done

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- report ----
# Join refs to status on (doc dir, token); awk keeps the doc order of pass 1.
awk -F'\t' '
  NR==FNR { st[$1 "\t" $2]=$3; next }
  { n=split($1, a, "/"); d=(n&gt;1) ? substr($1, 1, length($1)-length(a[n])-1) : ".";
    print $1 ":" $2 "  " $3 "\t" st[d "\t" $3] }' "$STATUS" "$REFS" &gt; "$TMP/joined"

CHECKED=$(wc -l &lt; "$REFS" | tr -d ' ')
awk -F'\t' '$2=="missing" { print $1 }' "$TMP/joined"
MISSING=$(awk -F'\t' '$2=="missing"' "$TMP/joined" | wc -l | tr -d ' ')
UNIQUE=$(awk -F'\t' '$3=="missing" { print $2 }' "$STATUS" | sort -u | wc -l | tr -d ' ')
echo ""
echo "MISSING: $MISSING reference(s) to $UNIQUE path(s) that do not exist, out of $CHECKED checked in $(wc -l &lt; "$TMP/files" | tr -d ' ') files."

if awk -F'\t' '$2=="untracked"' "$TMP/joined" | grep -q .; then
  echo ""
  echo "UNTRACKED (exists here, not in git: missing for a clone and for CI):"
  awk -F'\t' '$2=="untracked" { print "  " $1 }' "$TMP/joined"
fi

if [ "$RAW" != "1" ] &amp;&amp; [ -s "$FILTERED" ]; then
  echo ""
  echo "FILTERED as non-repo shapes ($(sort -u "$FILTERED" | wc -l | tr -d ' ') unique; RAW=1 checks them anyway):"
  sort -u "$FILTERED" | sed 's/^/  /'
fi

[ "$MISSING" -eq 0 ]
```</result>
~~~~

### Claude replied · 18:44:55 EDT

~~~~text
Live now: the corrected tokens and the new title are served. Everything from your message is done except the parts that need your hands, which I list below.

**The audit.** Four read-only agents audited every doc in disjoint sets and re-derived each claim by command; three editing agents applied about 150 drifts across 20 files, merged as [#38](https://github.com/Volee-Team/FXE/pull/38). The drift had four shapes: counts copied forward, status markers never moved after the work landed, decisions superseded without a note, and the SQL auditor agent still being Volee's file from August. The mechanism so it cannot recur: a doc-path checker now runs in CI and fails when a doc names a file that is not there. It found 19 such references on its first run.

**One real security finding came out of it.** The nightly backups sit as artifacts on a public repo, and any signed-in GitHub user can download them. They contain Tara's email and password hash. The workflow now encrypts each dump and refuses to upload without your key, so it will fail nightly until you do this once:

```bash
brew install age && age-keygen -o ~/fxe-backup-key.txt
```

Keep that file in your password manager, then paste the `age1...` public line from it into `.github/backup-recipient.txt` and push. Separately decide whether to delete the ten existing unencrypted artifacts (they expire in December) or make the repo private.

**The folder mistake.** You are right, and the fix is in: CLAUDE.md now points at the four docs inside the repo and records why they moved. Nothing was lost in the fork.

**The CI Supabase project.** Yes, it helps a lot: it is the only way to run the 13 phone UI tests on every PR instead of only on my Mac. It does not touch Volee. I checked the account today: two orgs, Volee and FXE, one project each. The free plan is two projects per org, so `fxe-ci` goes in the FXE org at no cost. You create it in the dashboard and set two GitHub secrets; I do the rest. Details are in section F of the launch checklist.

**Stripe, your exact steps.** They are in section B of [launch-checklist.md](docs/launch-checklist.md). In short:

1. Create the account at stripe.com and stay in test mode. No bank details needed for that.
2. Copy the publishable and secret test keys into Supabase Edge Function secrets as `STRIPE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` and `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY`. Never into chat or the repo.
3. Add a webhook endpoint pointing at the hosted `stripe-webhook` function with the five events listed there, and set its signing secret as `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET`.
4. Tell me it is done. I run the same 27-check harness against real Stripe with a test card.

Live mode later needs Tara's bank account and SSN or EIN, the one step nobody else can do.

**What is left to reach production.** The checklist has every row with an owner. The short version:

- Blocked on you: the Stripe keys, the age key, the CI project, custom SMTP for auth email, the Supabase plan decision.
- Blocked on Tara: questions 27 to 42 (the short list is ready to send, now 16 items), her real clinics in hosted, her two sentences.
- Blocked on Apple: enrollment, then the bundle id, push key, TestFlight.
- Mine, unblocked: account deletion in the app once Tara says what it deletes, crash reporting, the open review items now in the backlog.

**Testing, triple-checked.** Section E of the checklist lists every layer and every gap. The honest summary: probes, browser tests, unit tests, the Stripe harness, and the copy and secret gates run on every PR. The phone UI tests run only on my Mac until the CI project exists. Never done at all: a real-device pass, an accessibility pass, offline behaviour, a 60-clinic season on a phone, and an adversarial pass over the edge functions. The restore drill happened this morning and found a real bug in the backup, which is why drills exist.
~~~~

~~~~text
The EPIC repo is set up, the environment is verified against Emily's notebook, and the parity test is red for exactly one reason: her `biomechdata.py` is not on this Mac. This session now points at the new repo.

**What exists at `/Users/alex/Documents/GITHUB/epic-ml-opt`** (one commit, `71e1eec`):

- Both docs at the root, the ST08 checkpoint under `checkpoints/`, and Emily's notebook and config under `external/`, unedited. The copied checkpoint matches the Desktop original by sha256.
- A Python 3.11 virtualenv in `.venv` with the exact versions the notebook asserts. There is no conda on this Mac, so I used Homebrew's python3.11 instead, and pinned torchao to 0.17.0 because litert-torch pulled 0.18.0 on its own. The notebook's cell 0 asserts pass.
- `scripts/inspect_checkpoint.py`, which opens the checkpoint without needing the TCN class. Every claim in the handoff checked out against the file (5 blocks of 80, kernel 5, receptive field 249, eff_hist 248), with one correction: `center` and `scale` are shape (1, 12, 1), not (12,). That shape broadcasts straight onto a (batch, 12, time) input, which hints the model normalises inside its own forward. Only her file settles it.
- `tests/test_parity.py`, the torch-versus-LiteRT gate written first, per your TDD rule. Output of `.venv/bin/python -m pytest`:

```
ERROR tests/test_parity.py::test_torch_model_runs - Failed: external/biomechdata.py is missing
ERROR tests/test_parity.py::test_litert_matches_torch - Failed: external/biomechdata.py is missing
2 errors in 0.40s
```

It fails rather than skips on purpose. A skipped gate reads as green, and the whole point of a gate is that it cannot be quietly bypassed.

**Concept worth knowing:** `torch.load` and building the model are two separate steps. The checkpoint is just a dictionary of numbers and settings, so it opens fine without Emily's code. Only `TCN(**ckpt)` needs her class. That is why I could verify the file today and why the test fails at the fixture, not at load.

**What only you can do:**

1. Get `biomechdata.py` from Emily (or her whole `tcn-training-main` repo) and drop it in `/Users/alex/Documents/GITHUB/epic-ml-opt/external/`. If it is on the lab GitHub, the stored login for github.gatech.edu on this Mac has expired, so first run:

Once the file lands, the next step is running that test green, then `convert.py`, then the latency harness.
~~~~

### Claude replied · 21:45:51 EDT

~~~~text
No more edits. Here is the picture, from the top.

## The problem the lab is solving

A child with crouch gait wears a powered knee exoskeleton. For the motor to help at the right moment, the controller has to know where in the step the leg is right now: heel just struck, mid-stance, about to swing. That "where in the step" is **gait phase**, a number from 0% (heel strike) to 100% (the instant before the next heel strike). Roughly the first 60% is stance (foot on the ground) and the last 40% is swing.

Two small IMU sensors, one on the thigh and one on the shank, each give 6 numbers many times a second: acceleration in x, y, z and rotation rate in x, y, z. That is the 12 inputs. A neural network reads a short history of those 12 signals and outputs the phase.

## Why the output is two numbers, not one

Phase wraps around: 99% and 1% are physically next to each other but numerically far apart, so a network trained to output "percent" gets punished for a near-miss at the wrap. The fix is to put phase on a circle. The network outputs cos and sin of the phase (called gait_phase_x and gait_phase_y). You recover the percent with atan2(y, x). That is why the model has 2 outputs and why the "phase error" metric has to wrap into the range -50 to +50.

## The model Emily gave you

It is a **TCN**, a temporal convolutional network. Think of it as a stack of filters that slide along time, each looking at 5 consecutive samples. There are 5 stacked levels, each with 80 filters, and each level skips samples at twice the stride of the one below (dilation), so the top of the stack sees far back in time cheaply. The total lookback, the **receptive field**, works out to 249 samples. The checkpoint stores that number as 248, which is receptive field minus one, and yesterday's inspection confirmed the arithmetic against the file. It is causal, meaning it only looks backward, which is what a real-time controller needs.

It is small: about 296,000 parameters, 1.2 MB on disk. That matters for the plan, because a model this size may already run fast enough on a Pi, and you should measure before assuming inference is the slow part of the loop.

Two more facts about the checkpoint file itself:

- It is not a tar archive despite the name. It is PyTorch's own save format. It opens with `torch.load`, and it contains the recipe (layer sizes, kernel size, and per-channel normalisation values called center and scale) plus the weights.
- The recipe is useless without the kitchen. The class that turns the recipe into a runnable model is `TCN` inside a file called `biomechdata.py`, which lives in Emily's training repo and is not on your Mac. That is the single blocker.

## What you are being asked to do

Sidd split the work in two. Emily's side is finding the best small model (accuracy). Your side is making whatever she produces as fast as possible on the Pi, without degrading the estimate below what clinicians accept. Because her model may change, your tooling has to be model-agnostic: it takes a checkpoint as input and does not care what is inside.

His order of operations, and the reason for each step:

1. **Run the PyTorch model on the laptop and measure a baseline.** Latency per prediction and phase error. Everything later is compared to this.
2. **Convert to LiteRT** (the runtime formerly called TFLite, which is what runs on the Pi) and prove the converted model gives the same answers. This is the **parity test**, and it is written already. The lab used to think you had to go PyTorch to ONNX to TensorFlow to TFLite. That is outdated. Emily's own notebook converts directly, and it worked for a different model (a hip model called SM04). Your job is four small edits to adapt it: the filename, dropping a channel slice she needed and you do not, the output names, and the input shape (1, 12, 249).
3. **Quantize.** Turn 32-bit floating-point weights into 8-bit integers plus a scale factor. About 4 times smaller, faster integer math on the Pi's CPU, small precision loss. Re-run parity and phase error to see how much was lost.
4. **Only then, the Pi.** Laptop numbers are a correctness reference. Pi numbers are the real performance numbers. Never compare Mac milliseconds to Pi milliseconds.

## How results get recorded

Every configuration you try appends one row to a CSV: runtime, data type, latency at the 50th, 95th and 99th percentiles, phase error, model size, git commit. Percentiles rather than the mean because the exo cares about the worst tick, not the average. The end product of the semester is a plot of latency against phase error across everything tried. That plot is the paper figure.

## Where things stand

- Repo at `/Users/alex/Documents/GITHUB/epic-ml-opt`, one commit.
- Python environment built and matching Emily's notebook exactly. No conda on your Mac, so it is a plain virtualenv; the commands are in the README.
- The checkpoint has been opened and every claim in the handoff checked against it. One correction landed: center and scale are shaped (1, 12, 1), which hints the model normalises inputs itself. Emily's file will confirm.
- The parity test exists and fails, on purpose, because it cannot import her TCN class. That is the correct starting state for test-first work: the test describes the goal before any converter code exists.

## What I need from you

1. **Which GitHub, and the URL.** Your GitHub.com account has no EPIC repo, and the lab dashboard repo points at github.gatech.edu, where your stored login has expired. If Emily's `tcn-training-main` is there, run the command below and paste me the repo URL. If it is somewhere else, just paste the link.

```bash
gh auth login --hostname github.gatech.edu
```

2. **Four answers from Emily**, in plain words: does the model normalise inputs itself or must the caller do it; what is the sensor sample rate; what does a test file look like and does it include ground-truth phase; and what phase error do clinicians consider acceptable.

3. Optional but useful: which Raspberry Pi model the exo uses, so the eventual numbers are for the right chip.
~~~~

### Claude replied · 16:20:40 EDT

~~~~text
No code touched. The four zips are unpacked under `data/` in the EPIC repo, which is gitignored because they are patient recordings, and the findings are committed in [docs/data-notes-2026-09-20.md](/Users/alex/Documents/GITHUB/epic-ml-opt/docs/data-notes-2026-09-20.md).

## Where we are

Two of the four things on your "need from Emily" list are now answered by the data itself. Two are still open, and the data raised one new problem that matters more than any of them.

**1. Test file and columns: answered.** The stroke files (ST13, ST14) are exactly the file you asked her for. Each has `time`, the 12 sensor columns in the config's order, then `gait_phase_x` and `gait_phase_y` as ground truth. Six files per subject, about 7,500 rows each. The ground truth sits perfectly on the unit circle and maps to 0 to 100 percent with atan2, so the error metric Sidd wants can be computed from these directly. A few hundred rows per file have blank labels. Those rows get skipped when scoring, nothing else.

**2. Sample rate: answered.** Every file ticks at 0.005 seconds, so 200 Hz. The 249-sample window is therefore 1.25 seconds of history, roughly one stride. The on-device window and control loop rate are still hers to confirm.

**3. Normalisation: still open, and now more urgent.** The model stores a per-channel center and scale. For thigh acceleration the center is 1.83. The stroke data averages 9.4 on that channel, because it is in metres per second squared. The pediatric data averages 0.7, because it is in g. Neither matches 1.83, and 1.83 is what you get if the training set was mostly g with some m/s² mixed in. That would be a units mismatch inside the lab's own training pipeline. It does not affect parity, which is the torch-versus-LiteRT comparison and works on any input. It does affect any error number we report, because the model has to be fed what it was trained on. Ask Emily, plainly: were the stroke and kids' recordings converted to the same unit before training?

**4. The py file: still the blocker.** Nothing runs without it.

## Two things you did not ask about but should know

**The pediatric files are not test files.** GR04 and GR05 are raw exo logs: 48 columns of controller state, torques, magnetometer, quaternions, and the ground-truth phase columns are blank on 36 to 57 percent of rows. They are 250 to 650 MB each. Something in Emily's pipeline turns files like these into the clean 15-column stroke format, and we do not have that step. For now the stroke files are the ones to work with.

**These four subjects were probably in the training set.** The config lists ST13, ST14, GR04, GR05 and ST08 as subjects, with a "leave everyone else in" baseline group, and the checkpoint is named ST08. That pattern usually means ST08 was held out for testing and this model was trained on the rest. If so, an error number measured on these four will look better than the truth. Ask for ST08's own files.

## What I need from you

1. **Send Emily the eight numbered questions** at the bottom of the data notes doc. The ones that change what we build are the units question, whether ST08 is the held-out subject, and the py file.
2. **The GitHub link** for her `tcn-training-main` repo, if that is where the py file lives. Your github.gatech.edu login on this Mac has expired; if it is there, run this first:

```bash
gh auth login --hostname github.gatech.edu
```

3. Sidd's paper is on your list, not mine. Once the py file lands, the next session runs the parity test green, adapts the converter, and produces the first latency and error numbers on the stroke files.
~~~~

## 2026-09-20 23:15:15 EDT · `main`

### Claude replied · 23:15:49 EDT

~~~~text
Here is each question with the file it comes from, what I saw, and what goes wrong if we guess.

## 1. The `biomechdata.py` file

**Which file:** Emily's converter notebook, cell 1, has the line `from biomechdata import TCN`. The checkpoint she sent you is a dictionary of settings plus weights. The settings are things like "kernel size 5, five levels of 80 channels". The weights are the numbers the training produced. What is missing is the Python class that reads those settings and builds the actual network the weights plug into. That class is `TCN`, and it lives in `biomechdata.py` inside her `tcn-training-main` repo.

**Why we need it:** without it, nothing runs. Not the baseline, not the conversion, not a single latency number. I could write my own TCN class that matches the weight names, and it would probably load, but "probably" is the problem. If her forward pass does one thing differently from mine, say it normalises the input or reads the last timestep a different way, my model would produce different outputs from hers while looking healthy. Then every number we report would be about my model, not the lab's. This is the only question where the answer is a file, not a sentence.

## 2. Units: were the stroke and kids' data put in the same unit before training?

**Which files:** three places disagree with each other.

- The stroke test files, for example `ST13/normal_walk/ST13_normal_walk_1_0-3_processed.csv`. The `thigh_acc_x` column averages about 9.4. Gravity is 9.8 m/s², and a thigh sensor pointing along the leg reads mostly gravity, so these are in metres per second squared.
- The kids' files, for example `GR04/normal_walk/V1_final.csv`. The same column averages about 0.7 and never exceeds 8. That is the same physical signal in g, where gravity is 1.0.
- The checkpoint itself stores a `center` and `scale` for each of the 12 channels. These are the numbers the model subtracts and divides by before it looks at the data, computed from whatever the training set contained. For `thigh_acc_x` the center is 1.83. A training set entirely in m/s² would give roughly 9. Entirely in g would give roughly 1. A value of 1.83 is what you get when most of the data is in g and some is in m/s².

**Why we need it:** the model only knows the world it was trained on. If it was trained on a mix, feeding it clean m/s² stroke data means every input is shifted several standard deviations from what it expects, and the phase error we measure will be large for reasons that have nothing to do with the model or our conversion. If the training pipeline converts units in code, then her py file may answer this on its own and we do it the same way. If it was a genuine mix, that is a finding for Emily and Sidd, not for us to fix, but we still need to know so that we test with the same mix. This question does not affect parity, which only asks whether the converted model matches the original on the same input. It affects every accuracy number.

## 3. Is ST08 the held-out subject, and can we have its files?

**Which files:** the training config `StrokePeds_0806_config.py` lists the subjects the run used, and ST13, ST14, GR04, GR05 and ST08 are all on that list. It also sets `baseline_groups = ['ALL_OTHERS']`. The checkpoint is named `ST08_...`. The usual reading in this kind of lab is leave-one-subject-out: train on everyone except ST08, test on ST08, and name the result after the test subject.

**Why we need it:** if that reading is right, the four subjects she sent are ones the model already saw during training. Measuring error on them is like grading a student on the exact homework problems they practised. The number will be flattering and Sidd would rightly not trust it. For the first week it does not matter, because the first job is "does the converted model match the original", and any data works for that. It matters the moment we put an accuracy number in front of Sidd or in a paper. So ask, but it is not blocking.

## The ones I am not asking, and why

- **Does the model normalise inside itself or does the caller do it?** Her py file answers this directly, because the code either has a line that subtracts `center` and divides by `scale` or it does not. No need to ask a human.
- **Sample rate.** Every file steps by 0.005 seconds. That is 200 Hz. Answered by the data.
- **Window length the exo feeds the model.** The model's receptive field is 249 samples, and a longer window changes nothing about the output while a shorter one would be missing information. So 249 is the only sensible answer and I will use it. If the exo does something different it will show up when we integrate, and that is a conversation with Izabel, not Emily.
- **What `_mir` means, what `0-3` / `0-6` / `0-9` mean.** Every stroke file has a `_mir` twin where all 12 sensors and both labels differ, not by a sign flip, so it is probably the other leg mirrored to look like the instrumented one. The number suffixes are probably treadmill speeds. Curiosity, not blocking. Ask in person when you next talk.
- **How the kids' raw exo logs become clean test files.** The GR files have 48 columns including torque, magnetometer and quaternions, and the ground-truth phase is blank on 36 to 57 percent of rows. Something in her pipeline converts these into the 15-column stroke format. We do not need that step until we want kids' accuracy numbers, and by then she may just send processed versions.
- **What phase error is clinically acceptable.** Sidd's question, not Emily's, and it only matters once we have a number to hold against it.

So the text to Emily is still two items, the file and the units question, with the ST08 question as a third if it is easy to add.
~~~~

### Claude replied · 00:14:29 EDT

~~~~text
That was the file. Sidd's step 2 is done on the laptop: the model runs, converts to LiteRT directly with no ONNX hop, and the converted model gives the same answers.

**Results, from `.venv/bin/python -m pytest` in Desktop/EPIC ML:**

```
tests/test_parity.py ..    2 passed in 19.73s
```

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Every weight in the checkpoint matched the class | yes, no missing or unexpected keys |
| Torch vs LiteRT on the seeded input, max difference | 2.09e-07 (limit 1e-05) |
| Same test after nudging one bias by 0.001 | fails, as it must |
| LiteRT's own op count per 249-sample inference | 81 million multiply-adds |

The second row is the "prove the test can fail" step. A test that passes the first time you run it has not yet shown it can catch anything, so I corrupted one weight and confirmed it goes red. That habit is called mutation testing, and it is the cheapest insurance against a test that is green for the wrong reason.

**Two questions answered by the code, so Emily does not need to answer them:**

- **Normalisation happens inside the model.** Line 1079 of her file subtracts `center` and divides by `scale` before anything else. We feed raw sensor values. If we had normalised in the caller too, the data would have been normalised twice and every accuracy number would have been quietly wrong.
- **The model scores a whole trial in one pass.** Give it 1,000 samples and it returns 752 rows of output, one per timestep after the 248-sample warm-up. That makes computing phase error on a full ST13 file a single call rather than thousands of sliding windows.

**One thing to know:** her file imports a sibling called `biomechutils.py` that is in the same `src/` folder on the lab GitHub. The model class never uses it, so I put a clearly labelled stub in its place and the test swaps in the real file automatically if you ever download it. Not urgent.

**Still open with Emily:** only the units question you already sent. It does not block the next step.

**Next:** the baseline harness, `bench.py`. Latency at the 50th, 95th and 99th percentile for torch and LiteRT at batch size 1, plus phase error on the ST13 and ST14 files, each run writing one row to `results/bench.csv`. That gives Sidd the "execution time and performance compared to baseline" numbers he asked for first. Say the word and I build it.
~~~~
